I don't know, I still just can't get past it. I still firmly believe they're tracking my buys and messing with the numbers to make me look stupid, no matter what she thinks those reports prove.\n\nDoctor: Before all of this started happening at work, did you ever have beliefs like this — that people were tracking you or working against you — or is this something completely new for you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not completely new, but it hasn't been happening my whole life, either. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months. \n\nIt all started right after the company reorganized. We had this big exclusive sneaker launch, and they shifted things around, and... yeah, that's really when I first noticed they were tracking me and working against me.\n\nDoctor: Besides these beliefs about people tracking you and messing with your numbers, have you ever heard voices or seen things that others around you couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. I mean, I looked at the reports she pulled up, but... it didn't make me think I was wrong. Like I was just saying, I still can't shake the feeling that they're doing it. \n\nThe paperwork doesn't really change anything for me. I still firmly believe they're tracking my buys and messing with the numbers to make me look stupid, no matter what she thinks those reports prove.\n\nDoctor: Before all of this started happening at work, did you ever have beliefs like this — that people were tracking you or working against you — or is this something completely new for you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not completely new, but it hasn't been happening my whole life, either. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months. \n\nIt all started right after the company reorganized. We had this big exclusive sneaker launch, and they shifted things around, and... yeah, that's really when I first noticed they were tracking me and working against me.\n\nDoctor: Besides these beliefs about people tracking you and messing with your numbers, have you ever heard voices or seen things that others around you couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. Sometimes when I'm alone, like if I'm back in the stockroom or driving back from a vendor meeting, I hear this man's voice. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me. He'll say things like, 'Move faster, Scott,' or he'll just laugh at the picks I made for the buys. But whenever I turn around to see who it is, there's nobody there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thinking or the way you speak has become confused or disorganized, or have people told you that what you're saying doesn't make sense or is hard to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my thoughts feel pretty clear to me. I know what I want to say, and I just say it. And nobody at work or... anywhere else has ever told me I'm hard to understand or that I don't make sense. I feel like I communicate just fine.\n\nDoctor: Over this past year or so, have you noticed that you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or that you feel less motivated to do things, or that you're spending more time alone and withdrawing from friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I dodge texts from my friends when they ask me to play volleyball or go out to gallery nights. Honestly, it just feels like too much work to even type out an answer.\n\nDoctor: In that year or so before you first noticed people tracking you, did you go through any period where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like you had special abilities or insights that others didn't have?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't feel stiff or frozen, and I'm not doing anything weird with how I move. Nobody's said anything to me about acting strange or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voice you hear, have you ever felt like your thoughts weren't your own, or like someone was putting thoughts into your head, or broadcasting your thoughts so others could hear them?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that happen. My thoughts are just... my thoughts. I don't feel like anyone is putting them there or broadcasting them or anything. I still firmly believe they're tracking my buys and messing with the numbers to make me look stupid, no matter what she thinks those reports prove.\n\nDoctor: Before all of this started happening at work, did you ever have beliefs like this — that people were tracking you or working against you — or is this something completely new for you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not completely new, but it hasn't been happening my whole life, either. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months. \n\nIt all started right after the company reorganized. We had this big exclusive sneaker launch, and they shifted things around, and... yeah, that's really when I first noticed they were tracking me and working against me.\n\nDoctor: Besides these beliefs about people tracking you and messing with your numbers, have you ever heard voices or seen things that others around you couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. Sometimes when I'm alone, like if I'm back in the stockroom or driving back from a vendor meeting, I hear this man's voice. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me. He'll say things like, 'Move faster, Scott,' or he'll just laugh at the picks I made for the buys. But whenever I turn around to see who it is, there's nobody there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thinking or the way you speak has become confused or disorganized, or have people told you that what you're saying doesn't make sense or is hard to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my thoughts feel pretty clear to me. I know what I want to say, and I just say it. And nobody at work or... anywhere else has ever told me I'm hard to understand or that I don't make sense. I feel like I communicate just fine.\n\nDoctor: Over this past year or so, have you noticed that you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or that you feel less motivated to do things, or that you're spending more time alone and withdrawing from friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. On my off days, I mostly just sit around my apartment... I'll scroll through sneaker drops on my phone, but I don't really care about them anymore. And I've definitely been withdrawing a bit. I dodge texts from my friends when they ask me to play volleyball or go out to gallery nights. Honestly, it just feels like too much work to even type out an answer.\n\nDoctor: In that year or so before you first noticed people tracking you, did you go through any period where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like you had special abilities or insights that others didn't have?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's just there now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the way you move or act — like feeling frozen or stiff, or moving in unusual or repetitive ways — or has anyone commented on your behavior seeming odd or strange?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, what really made me come in today is... I just can't shake this feeling I've been having. I work as a buyer, and I'm pretty sure people at work, and maybe even some of the vendors, are tracking my buys. It feels like they're messing with the launch numbers on purpose just to make me look stupid. \n\nMy supervisor actually sat down and showed me the reports, and she says they don't prove any of that is happening, but... I don't know, I still just can't get past it. It's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: When your supervisor showed you those reports, did any part of you think 'okay, maybe I was wrong about this,' or do you still firmly believe something is going on despite what she showed you?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I looked at the reports she pulled up, but... it didn't make me think I was wrong. Like I was just saying, I still can't shake the feeling that they're doing it. \n\nThe paperwork doesn't really change anything for me. I still firmly believe they're tracking my buys and messing with the numbers to make me look stupid, no matter what she thinks those reports prove.\n\nDoctor: Before all of this started happening at work, did you ever have beliefs like this — that people were tracking you or working against you — or is this something completely new for you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not completely new, but it hasn't been happening my whole life, either. It's been going on for... I'd say about a year and three months. \n\nIt all started right after the company reorganized. We had this big exclusive sneaker launch, and they shifted things around, and... yeah, that's really when I first noticed they were tracking me and working against me.\n\nDoctor: Besides these beliefs about people tracking you and messing with your numbers, have you ever heard voices or seen things that others around you couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. Sometimes when I'm alone, like if I'm back in the stockroom or driving back from a vendor meeting, I hear this man's voice. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me. He'll say things like, 'Move faster, Scott,' or he'll just laugh at the picks I made for the buys. But whenever I turn around to see who it is, there's nobody there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thinking or the way you speak has become confused or disorganized, or have people told you that what you're saying doesn't make sense or is hard to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my thoughts feel pretty clear to me. I know what I want to say, and I just say it. And nobody at work or... anywhere else has ever told me I'm hard to understand or that I don't make sense. I feel like I communicate just fine.\n\nDoctor: Over this past year or so, have you noticed that you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or that you feel less motivated to do things, or that you're spending more time alone and withdrawing from friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. On my off days, I mostly just sit around my apartment... I'll scroll through sneaker drops on my phone, but I don't really care about them anymore. And I've definitely been withdrawing a bit. I dodge texts from my friends when they ask me to play volleyball or go out to gallery nights. Honestly, it just feels like too much work to even type out an answer.\n\nDoctor: In that year or so before you first noticed people tracking you, did you go through any period where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like you had special abilities or insights that others didn't have?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Before all the tracking stuff started, I felt pretty normal. I was sleeping my regular hours, mostly. I didn't have any, like, crazy bursts of energy or think I had special abilities or anything. It was just... just a normal year, really.\n\nDoctor: When you first started noticing people were tracking you about a year ago, did these beliefs and the voice you hear come on suddenly over days or weeks, or did they develop more gradually over a longer period of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. It's hard to put a timeline on it. I just... I know it's been happening for about a year, but I couldn't tell you if it started all at once or if it kind of built up over time. It's just there now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the way you move or act — like feeling frozen or stiff, or moving in unusual or repetitive ways — or has anyone commented on your behavior seeming odd or strange?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't feel stiff or frozen, and I'm not doing anything weird with how I move. Nobody's said anything to me about acting strange or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voice you hear, have you ever felt like your thoughts weren't your own, or like someone was putting thoughts into your head, or broadcasting your thoughts so others could hear them?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that happen. My thoughts are just... my thoughts. I don't feel like anyone is putting them there or broadcasting them or anything. It's really just the tracking and the voice.\n\nDoctor: When you first started noticing the tracking and hearing that voice about a year ago, were you already withdrawing from friends and losing interest in things, or did that withdrawal and loss of interest happen later on?\n\nPatient: I think the withdrawing kind of happened around the same time, maybe a bit after. I just... I don't really have the energy for things anymore. On my off days, I mostly just sit in my apartment. I'll scroll through sneaker drops on my phone, but I don't really care about them like I used to. And I dodge texts from my friends... they'll ask me to play volleyball or go to gallery nights, but it just feels like way too much work to even answer them, let alone actually go. I just stay in.\n\nDoctor: When you're sitting in your apartment on your days off, how do you feel emotionally? Do you feel sad or upset, or does it feel more like you're just... empty or numb, like you don't feel much of anything at all?\n\nPatient: I guess it's more like... I just feel kind of empty. I'm not really sitting there crying or feeling super sad or anything. It's just flat, you know? Like I'm just there, and I don't really feel much at all.\n\nDoctor: When you think about what's been happening over this past year — the tracking, the voice, all of it — which part bothers you the most or feels the most real to you: the belief that people are tracking and sabotaging your work, or the voice you hear in the stockroom and car?\n\nPatient: I think... it's definitely the tracking. That's actually what really made me come in. I just can't shake the feeling that people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor has sat down with me and shown me the reports, and he says they don't prove anything is happening, but... I don't know. It just feels so real to me. The voice is annoying, but the tracking is what's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, when you were first noticing the tracking and hearing the voice about a year ago, were you still enjoying things and going out with friends back then, or had you already started feeling empty and withdrawing even before the tracking started?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not entirely sure. It's a little hard to remember exactly how the timing lined up back then. I don't think I was feeling empty before the tracking started, though. Like I was saying earlier, I'm pretty sure me pulling away from friends and just feeling flat happened around the exact same time as the tracking and the voice, or maybe a little bit after. But before all that began... I don't really remember feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: When you first started noticing people were tracking you and hearing that voice, did it feel like something that came on pretty suddenly — like over just a few days or a week or two — or did it feel more like it was slowly creeping up on you over many weeks?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. It's kind of a blur. I don't remember if it was all of a sudden or if it just sort of built up slowly. I just know it started happening about a year ago.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the people at work tracking your buys and messing with the numbers, have you figured out exactly how they're doing it — like, do you have a clear picture of their plan or method — or is it more like you just know it's happening without being able to explain the details?\n\nPatient: I don't really know exactly how they're pulling it off, to be honest. I don't have, like, a specific diagram of their plan or anything. I just... I just know they're doing it. Like I said before, I can't shake the feeling that they're tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. Even when my supervisor sits me down and shows me the reports and says there's no proof, it doesn't change anything. I just know it's happening, even if I can't explain every little detail of how.\n\nDoctor: I know you've said it's hard to remember the exact timing, but let me ask it a different way: In that first month after the company reorganized — the month when you first noticed the tracking — did the voice and the beliefs feel like they were there from day one, or did they start small and then get stronger as the weeks went on?\n\nPatient: I really wish I could give you a clear answer on that, but... I honestly just don't know. Thinking back to that first month right after the reorganization, it's all just kind of mixed together in my head. \n\nI can't say for sure if the voice and the tracking stuff hit me on day one, or if it took a few weeks to get stronger. I just remember that by the time that month was over, it was definitely happening and I was dealing with it. But as for how it played out day by day... I really couldn't tell you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. harder than I mean to.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: When I'm keyed up I can't just
